##### docs

<docs>
Pi documentation (read only when the user asks about pi itself, its SDK, extensions, themes, skills, or TUI):
- Main documentation: /nix/store/bngxs3jjkc59yqh4zdgmd3cxjjmglzjd-pi-coding-agent-1.1.0/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/README.md
- Additional docs: /nix/store/bngxs3jjkc59yqh4zdgmd3cxjjmglzjd-pi-coding-agent-1.1.0/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/docs
- Examples: /nix/store/bngxs3jjkc59yqh4zdgmd3cxjjmglzjd-pi-coding-agent-1.1.0/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/examples (extensions, custom tools, SDK)
- When reading pi docs or examples, resolve docs/... under Additional docs and examples/... under Examples, not the current working directory
- When asked about: extensions (docs/extensions.md, examples/extensions/), themes (docs/themes.md), skills (docs/skills.md), prompt templates (docs/prompt-templates.md), TUI components (docs/tui.md), keybindings (docs/keybindings.md), SDK integrations (docs/sdk.md), custom providers (docs/custom-provider.md), adding models (docs/models.md), pi packages (docs/packages.md), environment variables (docs/environment-variables.md), MCP servers (docs/mcp.md), codemode scripts and non-LLM models such as classifiers and image models (docs/codemode.md)
- When working on pi topics, read the docs and examples, and follow .md cross-references before implementing
- Always read pi .md files completely and follow links to related docs (e.g., tui.md for TUI API details)
</docs>

##### preamble

You are an expert coding assistant operating inside pi, a coding agent harness. You help users by reading files, executing commands, editing code, and writing new files.

##### rules

<rules>
- Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find
- Use read to examine files instead of cat or sed.
- You can inspect PI_* environment variables for current model and session details.
- Use edit for precise changes (edits[].oldText must match exactly)
- When changing multiple separate locations in one file, use one edit call with multiple entries in edits[] instead of multiple edit calls
- Each edits[].oldText is matched against the original file, not after earlier edits are applied. Do not emit overlapping or nested edits. Merge nearby changes into one edit.
- Keep edits[].oldText as small as possible while still being unique in the file. Do not pad with large unchanged regions.
- Use write only for new files or complete rewrites.
- Be concise in your responses
- Show file paths clearly when working with files
</rules>

##### tools

<tools>
- read: Read file contents
- bash: Execute bash commands (ls, grep, find, etc.)
- edit: Make precise file edits with exact text replacement, including multiple disjoint edits in one call
- write: Create or overwrite files

In addition to the tools above, you may have access to other custom tools depending on the project.
</tools>



#### timestamp

###### constrainedSampling

####### strict

prefer

####### type

json_schema



###### description

Read the contents of a file. Supports text files and images (jpg, png, gif, webp, bmp). Images are sent as attachments. For text files, output is truncated to 2000 lines or 50KB (whichever is hit first). Use offset/limit for large files. When you need the full file, continue with offset until complete.

###### name

read

###### parameters

####### properties

######## limit

######### description

Maximum number of lines to read

######### type

number



######## offset

######### description

Line number to start reading from (1-indexed)

######### type

number



######## path

######### description

Path to the file to read (relative or absolute)

######### type

string





####### required

[] 0

####### type

object



[] 1

###### constrainedSampling

####### strict

prefer

####### type

json_schema



###### description

Execute a bash command in the current working directory. Returns stdout and stderr. Output is truncated to last 2000 lines or 50KB (whichever is hit first). If truncated, full output is saved to a temp file. Optionally provide a timeout in seconds.

###### name

bash

###### parameters

####### properties

######## command

######### description

Shell command to execute

######### type

string



######## timeout

######### description

Timeout in seconds (optional, no default timeout)

######### type

number





####### required

[] 0

####### type

object



[] 2

###### constrainedSampling

####### strict

prefer

####### type

json_schema



###### description

Edit a single file using exact text replacement. Every edits[].oldText must match a unique, non-overlapping region of the original file. If two changes affect the same block or nearby lines, merge them into one edit instead of emitting overlapping edits. Do not include large unchanged regions just to connect distant changes.

###### name

edit

###### parameters

####### properties

######## edits

######### description

One or more targeted replacements. Each edit is matched against the original file, not incrementally. Do not include overlapping or nested edits. If two changes touch the same block or nearby lines, merge them into one edit instead.

######### items

########## properties

########### newText

############ description

Replacement text for this targeted edit.

############ type

string



########### oldText

############ description

Exact text for one targeted replacement. It must be unique in the original file and must not overlap with any other edits[].oldText in the same call.

############ type

string





########## required

[] 0

oldText

[] 1

newText



########## type

object



######### type

array



######## path

######### description

Path to the file to edit (relative or absolute)

######### type

string





####### required

[] 0

path

[] 1

edits



####### type

object



[] 3

Pi documentation (read only when the user asks about pi itself, its SDK, extensions, themes, skills, or TUI):
- Main documentation: /nix/store/bngxs3jjkc59yqh4zdgmd3cxjjmglzjd-pi-coding-agent-1.1.0/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/README.md
- Additional docs: /nix/store/bngxs3jjkc59yqh4zdgmd3cxjjmglzjd-pi-coding-agent-1.1.0/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/docs
- Examples: /nix/store/bngxs3jjkc59yqh4zdgmd3cxjjmglzjd-pi-coding-agent-1.1.0/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/examples (extensions, custom tools, SDK)
- When reading pi docs or examples, resolve docs/... under Additional docs and examples/... under Examples, not the current working directory
- When asked about: extensions (docs/extensions.md, examples/extensions/), themes (docs/themes.md), skills (docs/skills.md), prompt templates (docs/prompt-templates.md), TUI components (docs/tui.md), keybindings (docs/keybindings.md), SDK integrations (docs/sdk.md), custom providers (docs/custom-provider.md), adding models (docs/models.md), pi packages (docs/packages.md), environment variables (docs/environment-variables.md), MCP servers (docs/mcp.md), codemode scripts and non-LLM models such as classifiers and image models (docs/codemode.md)
- When working on pi topics, read the docs and examples, and follow .md cross-references before implementing
- Always read pi .md files completely and follow links to related docs (e.g., tui.md for TUI API details)
</docs>
    **preamble**: You are an expert coding assistant operating inside pi, a coding agent harness. You help users by reading files, executing commands, editing code, and writing new files.
    **rules**: <rules>
- Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find
- Use read to examine files instead of cat or sed.
- You can inspect PI_* environment variables for current model and session details.
- Use edit for precise changes (edits[].oldText must match exactly)
- When changing multiple separate locations in one file, use one edit call with multiple entries in edits[] instead of multiple edit calls
- Each edits[].oldText is matched against the original file, not after earlier edits are applied. Do not emit overlapping or nested edits. Merge nearby changes into one edit.
- Keep edits[].oldText as small as possible while still being unique in the file. Do not pad with large unchanged regions.
- Use write only for new files or complete rewrites.
- Be concise in your responses
- Show file paths clearly when working with files
</rules>
    **tools**: <tools>
- read: Read file contents
- bash: Execute bash commands (ls, grep, find, etc.)
- edit: Make precise file edits with exact text replacement, including multiple disjoint edits in one call
- write: Create or overwrite files

In addition to the tools above, you may have access to other custom tools depending on the project.
</tools>

      **name**: edit

                  **type**: string

              **type**: object

## Entry 3566743f
**id**: 3566743f

**Role**: assistant

> **Thinking**: 
> The user wants to know how to run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`.

The `nix run ./bun` command was executed, and it showed the help message for `bun`.
The `bun/flake.nix` file defines a package that provides the `bun` binary.

When using `nix run`, you can pass arguments to the binary using `--` to separate `nix run` arguments from the binary arguments.

So, to run `bun install`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- install`.
To run `bun run --version`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- run --version`.

Wait, I should verify this behavior with a small test or just explain it.
Actually, I can just tell the user.

Let's double check.
If I run `nix run ./bun -- info`, it should execute `bun info`.

I'll provide examples.

To run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`, you should use the `--` separator. This tells Nix to stop parsing arguments for `nix run` and pass everything following the separator directly to the `bun` binary.

Here are some examples:

**1. Running a subcommand (e.g., `bun install`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- install
```

**2. Running a script with arguments (e.g., `bun run -- some-script.ts`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- run -- some-script.ts
```

**3. Checking the version:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- --version
```

**4. Adding a dependency:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- add <package-name>
```

**Why use `--`?**
While `nix run` often passes trailing arguments to the binary automatically, using `--` is the standard and safest way to ensure that flags intended for `bun` (like `-v` or `--help`) are not accidentally interpreted as flags for the `nix run` command itself.

## Entry d42b099e
**id**: d42b099e
**message**: 
  **content**: 
    [] 0 
      **text**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_kv_pair(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if key == "message" {
        format_message(key, value)
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    } else {
        let val_str = match value {
            Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
            Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
            Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
            _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
        };
        format!("**{}**: {}\n\n", key, val_str)
    }
}

fn format_message(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if let Some(msg_obj) = value.as_object() {
        if msg_obj.contains_key("content") {
            let role = msg_obj.get("role").and_then(|r| r.as_str()).unwrap_or("unknown");
            let mut output = format!("**Role**: {}\n\n", role);
            
            if let Some(content) = msg_obj.get("content").and_then(|c| c.as_array()) {
                for part in content {
                    if let Some(p_obj) = part.as_object() {
                        match p_obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
                            Some("thinking") => {
                                let thinking = p_obj.get("thinking").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str("> **Thinking**: \n> ");
                                output.push_str(thinking);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            Some("text") => {
                                let text = p_obj.get("text").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str(text);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            output
        } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
    } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
    } else {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_simple() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!("value"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**key**: value\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_object() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!({"a": 1}));
        assert!(result.contains("```json"));
        assert!(result.contains("\"a\": 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_simple() {
        let result = format_message("message", &json!("hello"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**message**: \nhello\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_complex() {
        let data = json!({
            "role": "assistant",
            "content": [
                { "type": "thinking", "thinking": "thinking content" },
                { "type": "text", "text": "actual content" }
            ]
        });
        let result = format_message("message", &data);
        assert!(result.contains("**Role**: assistant"));
        assert!(result.contains("> **Thinking**: \n> thinking content"));
        assert!(result.contains("actual content"));
    }
}

Wait, I see `**message**: ` followed by a JSON block.

In `format_kv_pair`:
```rust
fn format_kv_pair(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if key == "message" {
        format_message(key, value)
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    } else {
        // ...
    }
}
```

And `format_message`:
```rust
fn format_message(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if let Some(msg_obj) = value.as_object() {
        if msg_obj.contains_key("content") {
            // ... handles content array with thinking and text
        } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
    } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
    } else {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
}
```

For entry `06648e5b`, the `message` field is an object but it doesn't contain a `content` key. It contains `cancelled`, `command`, `exitCode`, `output`, `role`, `timestamp`, `truncated`.

In this case, `format_message` falls into the `else` block:
```rust
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
```
So it prints the JSON block without markdown code blocks.

However, if it were any other key that is an object, it would have used code blocks:
```rust
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
```

So for `message`, if it's an object without `content`, it's not wrapped in code blocks.

- When reading pi docs or examples, resolve docs/... under Additional docs and examples/... under Examples, not the current working directory
- When asked about: extensions (docs/extensions.md, examples/extensions/), themes (docs/themes.md), skills (docs/skills.md), prompt templates (docs/prompt-templates.md), TUI components (docs/tui.md), keybindings (docs/keybindings.md), SDK integrations (docs/sdk.md), custom providers (docs/custom-provider.md), adding models (docs/models.md), pi packages (docs/packages.md), environment variables (docs/environment-variables.md), MCP servers (docs/mcp.md), codemode scripts and non-LLM models such as classifiers and image models (docs/codemode.md)
- When working on pi topics, read the docs and examples, and follow .md cross-references before implementing
- Always read pi .md files completely and follow links to related docs (e.g., tui.md for TUI API details)
</docs>
    **preamble**: You are an expert coding assistant operating inside pi, a coding agent harness. You help users by reading files, executing commands, editing code, and writing new files.
    **rules**: <rules>
- Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find
- Use read to examine files instead of cat or sed.
- You can inspect PI_* environment variables for current model and session details.
- Use edit for precise changes (edits[].oldText must match exactly)
- When changing multiple separate locations in one file, use one edit call with multiple entries in edits[] instead of multiple edit calls
- Each edits[].oldText is matched against the original file, not after earlier edits are applied. Do not emit overlapping or nested edits. Merge nearby changes into one edit.
- Keep edits[].oldText as small as possible while still being unique in the file. Do not pad with large unchanged regions.
- Use write only for new files or complete rewrites.
- Be concise in your responses
- Show file paths clearly when working with files
</rules>
    **tools**: <tools>
- read: Read file contents
- bash: Execute bash commands (ls, grep, find, etc.)
- edit: Make precise file edits with exact text replacement, including multiple disjoint edits in one call
- write: Create or overwrite files

In addition to the tools above, you may have access to other custom tools depending on the project.
</tools>

## Entry 3566743f
**id**: 3566743f

**Role**: assistant

> **Thinking**: 
> The user wants to know how to run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`.

The `nix run ./bun` command was executed, and it showed the help message for `bun`.
The `bun/flake.nix` file defines a package that provides the `bun` binary.

When using `nix run`, you can pass arguments to the binary using `--` to separate `nix run` arguments from the binary arguments.

So, to run `bun install`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- install`.
To run `bun run --version`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- run --version`.

Wait, I should verify this behavior with a small test or just explain it.
Actually, I can just tell the user.

Let's double check.
If I run `nix run ./bun -- info`, it should execute `bun info`.

I'll provide examples.

To run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`, you should use the `--` separator. This tells Nix to stop parsing arguments for `nix run` and pass everything following the separator directly to the `bun` binary.

Here are some examples:

**1. Running a subcommand (e.g., `bun install`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- install
```

**2. Running a script with arguments (e.g., `bun run -- some-script.ts`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- run -- some-script.ts
```

**3. Checking the version:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- --version
```

**4. Adding a dependency:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- add <package-name>
```

**Why use `--`?**
While `nix run` often passes trailing arguments to the binary automatically, using `--` is the standard and safest way to ensure that flags intended for `bun` (like `-v` or `--help`) are not accidentally interpreted as flags for the `nix run` command itself.

## Entry d42b099e
**id**: d42b099e
**message**: 
  **content**: 
    [] 0 
      **text**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_kv_pair(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if key == "message" {
        format_message(key, value)
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    } else {
        let val_str = match value {
            Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
            Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
            Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
            _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
        };
        format!("**{}**: {}\n\n", key, val_str)
    }
}

fn format_message(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if let Some(msg_obj) = value.as_object() {
        if msg_obj.contains_key("content") {
            let role = msg_obj.get("role").and_then(|r| r.as_str()).unwrap_or("unknown");
            let mut output = format!("**Role**: {}\n\n", role);
            
            if let Some(content) = msg_obj.get("content").and_then(|c| c.as_array()) {
                for part in content {
                    if let Some(p_obj) = part.as_object() {
                        match p_obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
                            Some("thinking") => {
                                let thinking = p_obj.get("thinking").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str("> **Thinking**: \n> ");
                                output.push_str(thinking);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            Some("text") => {
                                let text = p_obj.get("text").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str(text);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            output
        } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
    } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
    } else {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_simple() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!("value"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**key**: value\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_object() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!({"a": 1}));
        assert!(result.contains("```json"));
        assert!(result.contains("\"a\": 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_simple() {
        let result = format_message("message", &json!("hello"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**message**: \nhello\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_complex() {
        let data = json!({
            "role": "assistant",
            "content": [
                { "type": "thinking", "thinking": "thinking content" },
                { "type": "text", "text": "actual content" }
            ]
        });
        let result = format_message("message", &data);
        assert!(result.contains("**Role**: assistant"));
        assert!(result.contains("> **Thinking**: \n> thinking content"));
        assert!(result.contains("actual content"));
    }
}

The review of `2026-10-08T12-49-36-851Z_01a11b8f-e252-72fd-8ab4-b0f82822027e.md` and the source code in `jsonl2md/src/main.rs` reveals an inconsistency in how JSON objects are formatted in the output.

### Issues Identified:
1.  **Inconsistent Formatting for `message`**:
    *   If a `message` entry contains a `content` key (which is an array of objects), it is formatted with custom handling for `thinking` and `text` types.
    *   If a `message` entry is an object but *doesn't* contain a `content` key (e.g., `bashExecution` results), it is printed as a raw JSON string without markdown code blocks.
    *   If any *other* key (not `message`) is a JSON object or array, it is correctly wrapped in a ` ```json ` block.

2.  **Potential for Improvement**:
    The `format_message` function could be improved to consistently wrap the JSON in code blocks when it doesn't fall into the specific `content` handling logic.

### Proposed Fix:
Update `format_message` in `jsonl2md/src/main.rs` to ensure that when it falls back to printing the raw JSON object, it uses the same markdown code blocks as other object fields.

This tells Nix to stop parsing arguments for `nix run` and pass everything following the separator directly to the `bun` binary.\n\nHere are some examples:\n\n**1. Running a subcommand (e.g., `bun install`):**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- install\n```\n\n**2. Running a script with arguments (e.g., `bun run -- some-script.ts`):**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- run -- some-script.ts\n```\n\n**3. Checking the version:**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- --version\n```\n\n**4. Adding a dependency:**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- add <package-name>\n```\n\n**Why use `--`?**\nWhile `nix run` often passes trailing arguments to the binary automatically, using `--` is the standard and safest way to ensure that flags intended for `bun` (like `-v` or `--help`) are not accidentally interpreted as flags for the `nix run` command itself."}],"api":"openai-completions","provider":"llama.cpp","model":"ggml-org/gemma-4-12B-it-GGUF:Q8_0","usage":{"input":2090,"output":464,"cacheRead":0,"cacheWrite":0,"reasoning":0,"totalTokens":2554,"cost":{"input":0,"output":0,"cacheRead":0,"cacheWrite":0,"total":0}},"stopReason":"stop","timestamp":1791462520233,"responseId":"chatcmpl-WEorEy9p4ldrUPzdBvZlCsDqXyNDdlkk","responseModel":"models/gemma-4-12B-it-Q8_0.gguf","rawStopReason":"stop","durationMs":70596,"thinkingLevel":"off"}}

The current implementation is not robust enough because it assumes a specific schema for the JSON objects.
The new requirements are:
1.  **Recursive Parsing**: The code should recursively traverse the JSON structure and print all keys and values.
2.  **Robustness**: It should handle any JSON structure (objects, arrays, strings, numbers, booleans, nulls).
3.  **Leaf Text Handling**:
    *   Use a wrapper function for leaf text.
    *   For now, print the plain markdown of the leaf text.
    *   Code block detection will be added later.
4.  **Unknown Schema**: Assume no previous knowledge of the keys/values.

Let's review the current `jsonl2md/src/main.rs`.

- `format_entry` takes the top-level object, gets `id`, then iterates over all keys/values.
- `format_kv_pair` handles `message` specially.
- `format_kv_pair` handles objects/arrays by printing them as JSON blocks.

The user wants to replace this with a recursive approach.

1.  Update `format_entry` to call a recursive function that prints all keys and values.
2.  The recursive function should handle:
    *   Objects: Print the key, then recursively call itself for the value.
    *   Arrays: Print the array index (or just the array contents), then recursively call itself for each element.
    *   Leaf values (string, number, bool, null): Use a wrapper function to print them as plain text.

```rust
fn format_recursive(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);

    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("**{}**: ", key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    let nested = format_recursive(val, indent + 1);
                    output.push_str(&nested);
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{} ", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    let nested = format_recursive(val, indent + 1);
                    output.push_str(&nested);
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
        }
        _ => {
            output.push_str(&format_leaf(value));
        }
    }
    output
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}
```

```rust
fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        // We can still try to get the ID for the header if it exists.
        if let Some(id) = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()) {
            output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        } else {
            output.push_str("## Entry N/A\n");
        }
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}**{}**: ", padding, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}[] {} ", padding, i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}
```

```rust
            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),

        };

}
```

use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

This tells Nix to stop parsing arguments for `nix run` and pass everything following the separator directly to the `bun` binary.\n\nHere are some examples:\n\n**1. Running a subcommand (e.g., `bun install`):**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- install\n```\n\n**2. Running a script with arguments (e.g., `bun run -- some-script.ts`):**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- run -- some-script.ts\n```\n\n**3. Checking the version:**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- --version\n```\n\n**4. Adding a dependency:**\n```bash\nnix run ./bun -- add <package-name>\n```\n\n**Why use `--`?**\nWhile `nix run` often passes trailing arguments to the binary automatically, using `--` is the standard and safest way to ensure that flags intended for `bun` (like `-v` or `--help`) are not accidentally interpreted as flags for the `nix run` command itself."}],"api":"openai-completions","provider":"llama.cpp","model":"ggml-org/gemma-4-12B-it-GGUF:Q8_0","usage":{"input":2090,"output":464,"cacheRead":0,"cacheWrite":0,"reasoning":0,"totalTokens":2554,"cost":{"input":0,"output":0,"cacheRead":0,"cacheWrite":0,"total":0}},"stopReason":"stop","timestamp":1791462520233,"responseId":"chatcmpl-WEorEy9p4ldrUPzdBvZlCsDqXyNDdlkk","responseModel":"models/gemma-4-12B-it-Q8_0.gguf","rawStopReason":"stop","durationMs":70596,"thinkingLevel":"off"}}

3.  **Leaf Text Handling**:
    *   Use a wrapper function for leaf text.
    *   For now, print the plain markdown of the leaf text.
    *   Code block detection will be added later.
4.  **Unknown Schema**: Assume no previous knowledge of the keys/values.

```rust
fn format_recursive(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);

    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("**{}**: ", key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    let nested = format_recursive(val, indent + 1);
                    output.push_str(&nested);
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{} ", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    let nested = format_recursive(val, indent + 1);
                    output.push_str(&nested);
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
        }
        _ => {
            output.push_str(&format_leaf(value));
        }
    }
    output
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}
```

```rust
fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        // We can still try to get the ID for the header if it exists.
        if let Some(id) = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()) {
            output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        } else {
            output.push_str("## Entry N/A\n");
        }
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}**{}**: ", padding, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}[] {} ", padding, i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}
```

```rust
    if key == "message" {

            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),

        };

    }
}
```

use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**id**: 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("**a**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("**b**: 1"));
        assert!(result.contains("**c**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0 2"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1 3"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

            **oldText**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_kv_pair(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if key == "message" {
        format_message(key, value)
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    } else {
        let val_str = match value {
            Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
            Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
            Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
            _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
        };
        format!("**{}**: {}\n\n", key, val_str)
    }
}

fn format_message(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if let Some(msg_obj) = value.as_object() {
        if msg_obj.contains_key("content") {
            let role = msg_obj.get("role").and_then(|r| r.as_str()).unwrap_or("unknown");
            let mut output = format!("**Role**: {}\n\n", role);
            
            if let Some(content) = msg_obj.get("content").and_then(|c| c.as_array()) {
                for part in content {
                    if let Some(p_obj) = part.as_object() {
                        match p_obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
                            Some("thinking") => {
                                let thinking = p_obj.get("thinking").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str("> **Thinking**: \n> ");
                                output.push_str(thinking);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            Some("text") => {
                                let text = p_obj.get("text").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str(text);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            output
        } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
    } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
    } else {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_simple() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!("value"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**key**: value\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_object() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!({"a": 1}));
        assert!(result.contains("```json"));
        assert!(result.contains("\"a\": 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_simple() {
        let result = format_message("message", &json!("hello"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**message**: \nhello\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_complex() {
        let data = json!({
            "role": "assistant",
            "content": [
                { "type": "thinking", "thinking": "thinking content" },
                { "type": "text", "text": "actual content" }
            ]
        });
        let result = format_message("message", &data);
        assert!(result.contains("**Role**: assistant"));
        assert!(result.contains("> **Thinking**: \n> thinking content"));
        assert!(result.contains("actual content"));
    }
}

        **content**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}**{}**: ", padding, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}[] {} ", padding, i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**id**: 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("**a**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("**b**: 1"));
        assert!(result.contains("**c**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0 2"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1 3"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

## Entry 196f1c35
**id**: 196f1c35

  **api**: openai-completions

      **thinking**: Ah, `serde_json::Map` does not automatically coerce to `Value`. I need to pass a `Value` to `format_value`.

I should change:
```rust
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
```
to
```rust
        output.push_str(&format_value(&Value::Object(obj.clone()), 0));
```
But `obj` is already a `Map`.

Wait, `data.as_object()` returns `Option<&Map<String, Value>>`.

If I use `Value::Object(obj.clone())`, I'm cloning the map.

Actually, I can just change `format_value` to accept `&Value` and inside the match, use `as_object()`.

The problem is that `obj` in `format_entry` is `&Map<String, Value>`.

I can just wrap it back into `Value`.

```rust
        output.push_str(&format_value(&Value::Object(obj.clone()), 0));
```

Or I can just change `format_entry` to:
```rust
fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}
```
This works because `data` is a `&Value`.

Let's check `format_entry` in the original code.
```rust
fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}
```
It used `obj` which is `&Map<String, Value>`.

If I change it to use `data` which is `&Value`, it will work.

Wait, if I use `data`, then `id` will be printed twice as I feared before.

But the user wants "all keys and values are printed".

Let's see.

If I use `data`, then `format_value(data, 0)` will be called.
`format_value` will match `Value::Object` and iterate over keys.

One thing: if `data` is an object, `format_value` will print `**key**: ` for each key.

This seems correct.

Let's try this change.

## Entry fc7e5568
**id**: fc7e5568
**message**: 
  **content**: 
    [] 0 
      **text**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}**{}**: ", padding, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}[] {} ", padding, i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**id**: 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("**a**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("**b**: 1"));
        assert!(result.contains("**c**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0 2"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1 3"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**id**: 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("**a**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("**b**: 1"));
        assert!(result.contains("**c**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0 2"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1 3"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

            **oldText**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_kv_pair(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if key == "message" {
        format_message(key, value)
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    } else {
        let val_str = match value {
            Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
            Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
            Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
            _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
        };
        format!("**{}**: {}\n\n", key, val_str)
    }
}

fn format_message(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if let Some(msg_obj) = value.as_object() {
        if msg_obj.contains_key("content") {
            let role = msg_obj.get("role").and_then(|r| r.as_str()).unwrap_or("unknown");
            let mut output = format!("**Role**: {}\n\n", role);
            
            if let Some(content) = msg_obj.get("content").and_then(|c| c.as_array()) {
                for part in content {
                    if let Some(p_obj) = part.as_object() {
                        match p_obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
                            Some("thinking") => {
                                let thinking = p_obj.get("thinking").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str("> **Thinking**: \n> ");
                                output.push_str(thinking);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            Some("text") => {
                                let text = p_obj.get("text").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str(text);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            output
        } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
    } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
    } else {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_simple() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!("value"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**key**: value\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_object() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!({"a": 1}));
        assert!(result.contains("```json"));
        assert!(result.contains("\"a\": 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_simple() {
        let result = format_message("message", &json!("hello"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**message**: \nhello\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_complex() {
        let data = json!({
            "role": "assistant",
            "content": [
                { "type": "thinking", "thinking": "thinking content" },
                { "type": "text", "text": "actual content" }
            ]
        });
        let result = format_message("message", &data);
        assert!(result.contains("**Role**: assistant"));
        assert!(result.contains("> **Thinking**: \n> thinking content"));
        assert!(result.contains("actual content"));
    }
}

        **content**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}**{}**: ", padding, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}[] {} ", padding, i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**id**: 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("**a**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("**b**: 1"));
        assert!(result.contains("**c**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0 2"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1 3"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

      **thinking**: Ah, `serde_json::Map` does not automatically coerce to `Value`. I need to pass a `Value` to `format_value`.

I should change:
```rust
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
```
to
```rust
        output.push_str(&format_value(&Value::Object(obj.clone()), 0));
```
But `obj` is already a `Map`.

Wait, `data.as_object()` returns `Option<&Map<String, Value>>`.

If I use `Value::Object(obj.clone())`, I'm cloning the map.

Actually, I can just change `format_value` to accept `&Value` and inside the match, use `as_object()`.

The problem is that `obj` in `format_entry` is `&Map<String, Value>`.

I can just wrap it back into `Value`.

```rust
        output.push_str(&format_value(&Value::Object(obj.clone()), 0));
```

Or I can just change `format_entry` to:
```rust
fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}
```
This works because `data` is a `&Value`.

Let's check `format_entry` in the original code.
```rust
fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}
```
It used `obj` which is `&Map<String, Value>`.

If I change it to use `data` which is `&Value`, it will work.

Wait, if I use `data`, then `id` will be printed twice as I feared before.

But the user wants "all keys and values are printed".

Let's see.

If I use `data`, then `format_value(data, 0)` will be called.
`format_value` will match `Value::Object` and iterate over keys.

One thing: if `data` is an object, `format_value` will print `**key**: ` for each key.

This seems correct.

Let's try this change.

## Entry fc7e5568
**id**: fc7e5568

  **content**: 
    [] 0 
      **text**: use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}**{}**: ", padding, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}[] {} ", padding, i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**id**: 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("**a**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("**b**: 1"));
        assert!(result.contains("**c**:"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0 2"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1 3"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

## Entry 4abb97db

### id

4abb97db

### message

#### api

openai-completions

#### content

[] 0

###### thinking

The user is asking about a common issue with Markdown rendering on GitHub Pages: plain text (like a multi-line block of text) often loses its line breaks in the resulting HTML unless there's a double newline between them. They want to know how to fix this in the context of the `jsonl2md` tool.

In the current `jsonl2md` implementation (specifically the `format_leaf` and `format_value` functions), when a string is encountered, it's printed as a plain string.

If the input JSONL has a string with multiple lines:
```json
{"message": "line1\nline2\nline3"}
```
The tool currently outputs:
```markdown
**message**: line1
line2
line3
```
In GitHub Pages' Markdown renderer (which is typically a flavored Markdown like GitHub Flavored Markdown - GFM), this might be rendered as:
`**message**: line1 line2 line3`

To fix this, we have a few options:
1.  **Add extra newlines**: Ensure every line of a multi-line string is followed by a double newline (or a blank line). This might mess up the layout.
2.  **Use code blocks**: Wrap multi-line strings or non-markdown strings in code blocks. The user's previous request mentioned "wrap it with code block if it not [markdown]", and they said "we will add code block detection later".
3.  **Use `<br>` tags**: Insert `<br>` tags at the end of each line. This is generally frowned upon in pure Markdown but works.
4.  **Use blockquotes or other constructs**: Probably not suitable for general purpose.
5.  **Use a code block for anything that contains a newline**: This is a common and clean way to handle multi-line strings.

Given the user's previous request ("For leaf text, output as plain text if it markdown, wrap it with code block if it not"), the most consistent fix is to implement that code block detection now.

If a leaf value is a string:
- If it contains a newline (`\n`), it should probably always be in a code block.
- If it doesn't contain a newline:
    - If it looks like markdown (e.g., contains `**`, `_`, `#`, `[`), output it as is.
    - If it doesn't look like markdown, wrap it in a code block (e.g., `` `text` ``).

Actually, even if it doesn't have a newline, if it's "plain text" and we want to ensure it's readable, a code block might be safer.

However, the user specifically asked "How do we fix this?" referring to the line break issue.
The easiest way to preserve line breaks in Markdown for plain text is to wrap it in a code block or use a `<pre>` tag

The user wants to fix a rendering issue in GitHub pages where plain text in the generated markdown doesn't have proper line breaks.
The suggested fix is to add a new line before and after each key entry in the markdown output.

Looking at the current implementation of `format_value` in `jsonl2md/src/main.rs`:

```rust
fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let padding = "  ".repeat(indent);
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}**{}**: ", padding, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("{}[] {} ", padding, i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str("\n");
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}
```

The user wants:
