###### text

To improve readability for human users on GitHub Pages, I will modify the rendering logic to use Markdown headers for all keys. This will ensure that each key and its value are clearly separated and that the structure of the JSON is preserved through nested header levels.

I will:
1. Update `format_entry` to provide a level-2 header for the entry ID.
2. Redesign `format_value` to be recursive, using headers for keys with a level determined by the nesting depth.
3. Remove the previous `format_kv_pair` and `format_message` functions as they are no longer needed for the new generic header-based approach.
4. Ensure value blocks are not indented, as requested.

```rust
use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}
```

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_kv_pair(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if key == "message" {
        format_message(key, value)
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    } else {
        let val_str = match value {
            Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
            Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
            Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
            _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
        };
        format!("**{}**: {}\n\n", key, val_str)
    }
}

fn format_message(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if let Some(msg_obj) = value.as_object() {
        if msg_obj.contains_key("content") {
            let role = msg_obj.get("role").and_then(|r| r.as_str()).unwrap_or("unknown");
            let mut output = format!("**Role**: {}\n\n", role);
            
            if let Some(content) = msg_obj.get("content").and_then(|c| c.as_array()) {
                for part in content {
                    if let Some(p_obj) = part.as_object() {
                        match p_obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
                            Some("thinking") => {
                                let thinking = p_obj.get("thinking").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str("> **Thinking**: \n> ");
                                output.push_str(thinking);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            Some("text") => {
                                let text = p_obj.get("text").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str(text);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            output
        } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
    } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
    } else {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_simple() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!("value"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**key**: value\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_object() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!({"a": 1}));
        assert!(result.contains("```json"));
        assert!(result.contains("\"a\": 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_simple() {
        let result = format_message("message", &json!("hello"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**message**: \nhello\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_complex() {
        let data = json!({
            "role": "assistant",
            "content": [
                { "type": "thinking", "thinking": "thinking content" },
                { "type": "text", "text": "actual content" }
            ]
        });
        let result = format_message("message", &data);
        assert!(result.contains("**Role**: assistant"));
        assert!(result.contains("> **Thinking**: \n> thinking content"));
        assert!(result.contains("actual content"));
    }
}

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}


######### oldText

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n", id));
        
        for (key, value) in obj {
            output.push_str(&format_kv_pair(key, value));
        }
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_kv_pair(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if key == "message" {
        format_message(key, value)
    } else if value.is_object() || value.is_array() {
        format!("**{}**: \n```json\n{}```\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    } else {
        let val_str = match value {
            Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
            Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
            Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
            Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
            _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
        };
        format!("**{}**: {}\n\n", key, val_str)
    }
}

fn format_message(key: &str, value: &Value) -> String {
    if let Some(msg_obj) = value.as_object() {
        if msg_obj.contains_key("content") {
            let role = msg_obj.get("role").and_then(|r| r.as_str()).unwrap_or("unknown");
            let mut output = format!("**Role**: {}\n\n", role);
            
            if let Some(content) = msg_obj.get("content").and_then(|c| c.as_array()) {
                for part in content {
                    if let Some(p_obj) = part.as_object() {
                        match p_obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
                            Some("thinking") => {
                                let thinking = p_obj.get("thinking").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str("> **Thinking**: \n> ");
                                output.push_str(thinking);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            Some("text") => {
                                let text = p_obj.get("text").and_then(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
                                output.push_str(text);
                                output.push_str("\n\n");
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            output
        } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
        } else {
            format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
        }
    } else if let Some(msg_str) = value.as_str() {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, msg_str)
    } else {
        format!("**{}**: \n{}\n\n", key, serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).unwrap())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("**key**: value"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_simple() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!("value"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**key**: value\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_kv_pair_object() {
        let result = format_kv_pair("key", &json!({"a": 1}));
        assert!(result.contains("```json"));
        assert!(result.contains("\"a\": 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_simple() {
        let result = format_message("message", &json!("hello"));
        assert_eq!(result, "**message**: \nhello\n\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_message_complex() {
        let data = json!({
            "role": "assistant",
            "content": [
                { "type": "thinking", "thinking": "thinking content" },
                { "type": "text", "text": "actual content" }
            ]
        });
        let result = format_message("message", &data);
        assert!(result.contains("**Role**: assistant"));
        assert!(result.contains("> **Thinking**: \n> thinking content"));
        assert!(result.contains("actual content"));
    }
}

The `edit` tool failed because the `oldText` provided was slightly different from what was actually in the file (likely due to line endings or invisible characters).

I will now use the `write` tool to overwrite `jsonl2md/src/main.rs` with the correct implementation. This is cleaner since the change is substantial.

The new implementation:
- Uses headers for all keys.
- Header level is determined by nesting depth (starting at level 3 for the first level of keys under a `## Entry` header).
- Value blocks are printed without indentation.
- Array indices are printed as `[] index`.
- `format_entry` still extracts the `id` for the `## Entry` header but then calls the recursive `format_value` on the entire object to ensure all keys/values are rendered.
- `format_kv_pair` and `format_message` are removed.

I'll also make sure to include the test cases.

####### content

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

###### text

The implementation has been updated to use Markdown headers for all JSON keys.

### Changes made:
- **Header-based rendering**: Each key in the JSON object is now rendered as a Markdown header. The header level automatically adjusts based on the nesting depth (starting at level 3).
- **Recursive processing**: The `format_value` function now recursively traverses the JSON structure, ensuring all nested objects and arrays are captured and rendered with correct header levels.
- **Simplified layout**: Indentation for values has been removed to improve readability on GitHub Pages, as requested.
- **Cleaned up code**: Removed the specialized `format_message` and `format_kv_pair` functions in favor of the more robust and generic recursive approach.
- **Refined `format_entry`**: The entry header (`## Entry id`) is preserved, and the rest of the object is rendered via the new recursive logic.

###### thinking

The user is reporting a compilation error in the `jsonl2md` project.
The error message is:
`error[E0308]: mismatched types`
`--> src/main.rs:36:39`
`36 |         output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));`
`|                          ------------ ^^^ expected &Value, found &Map<String, Value>`

The issue is that `obj` (which is obtained from `data.as_object()`) is a `&Map<String, Value>`, but `format_value` expects a `&Value`.

I need to fix `format_entry` to pass a `&Value` to `format_value`.

In the current `jsonl2md/src/main.rs`:
```rust
fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}
```

I should change `output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));` to `output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));` since `data` is already a `&Value`.

Wait, if I use `data`, it will include the `id` key again in the output below the `## Entry id` header.

Alternatively, I can wrap `obj` back into a `Value`.
Since `obj` is `&Map<String, Value>`, I can't easily turn it into `&Value` without cloning.

If I clone it:
`output.push_str(&format_value(&Value::Object(obj.clone()), 0));`

But `obj.clone()` clones the whole map.

Actually, `data` *is* the object we want to render. If I use `data` instead of `obj`, it will render everything, including the `id` key.

The user's request was: "Make it simple. Make sure each key is output as an markdown header with the proper level."

If the JSON is:
```json
{
  "id": "123",
  "type": "session"
}
```
The output should be:
```markdown
## Entry 123

### id
123

### type
session

---
```
This is perfectly fine and satisfies "all keys and values are printed".

So I will change `obj` to `data` in the `format_entry` function.

Let's check `jsonl2md/src/main.rs` first.

###### text

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(obj, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

```rust
fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
```

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (key, val) in map {
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

######### newText

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}


######### oldText

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;

    #[test]
    fn test_format_entry_basic() {
        let data = json!({
            "id": "123",
            "key": "value"
        });
        let result = format_entry(&data);
        assert!(result.contains("## Entry 123"));
        assert!(result.contains("### id"));
        assert!(result.contains("### key"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_value_recursive() {
        let data = json!({
            "a": {
                "b": 1
            },
            "c": [2, 3]
        });
        let result = format_value(&data, 0);
        assert!(result.contains("### a"));
        assert!(result.contains("#### b"));
        assert!(result.contains("### c"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 0"));
        assert!(result.contains("[] 1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_format_leaf() {
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!("hello")), "hello");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(42)), "42");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(true)), "true");
        assert_eq!(format_leaf(&json!(null)), "null");
    }
}

####### content

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

#5 preamble

You are an expert coding assistant operating inside pi, a coding agent harness. You help users by reading files, executing commands, editing code, and writing new files.

#5 rules

<rules>
- Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find
- Use read to examine files instead of cat or sed.
- You can inspect PI_* environment variables for current model and session details.
- Use edit for precise changes (edits[].oldText must match exactly)
- When changing multiple separate locations in one file, use one edit call with multiple entries in edits[] instead of multiple edit calls
- Each edits[].oldText is matched against the original file, not after earlier edits are applied. Do not emit overlapping or nested edits. Merge nearby changes into one edit.
- Keep edits[].oldText as small as possible while still being unique in the file. Do not pad with large unchanged regions.
- Use write only for new files or complete rewrites.
- Be concise in your responses
- Show file paths clearly when working with files
</rules>

#5 tools

<tools>
- read: Read file contents
- bash: Execute bash commands (ls, grep, find, etc.)
- edit: Make precise file edits with exact text replacement, including multiple disjoint edits in one call
- write: Create or overwrite files

In addition to the tools above, you may have access to other custom tools depending on the project.
</tools>

#6 thinking

The user wants to know how to run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`.

The `nix run ./bun` command was executed, and it showed the help message for `bun`.
The `bun/flake.nix` file defines a package that provides the `bun` binary.

When using `nix run`, you can pass arguments to the binary using `--` to separate `nix run` arguments from the binary arguments.

So, to run `bun install`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- install`.
To run `bun run --version`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- run --version`.

Wait, I should verify this behavior with a small test or just explain it.

#6 thinkingSignature

#6 type

[] 1

#6 text

To run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`, you should use the `--` separator. This tells Nix to stop parsing arguments for `nix run` and pass everything following the separator directly to the `bun` binary.

Here are some examples:

**1. Running a subcommand (e.g., `bun install`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- install
```

**2. Running a script with arguments (e.g., `bun run -- some-script.ts`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- run -- some-script.ts
```

**3. Checking the version:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- --version
```

**4. Adding a dependency:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- add <package-name>
```

**Why use `--`?**
While `nix run` often passes trailing arguments to the binary automatically, using `--` is the standard and safest way to ensure that flags intended for `bun` (like `-v` or `--help`) are not accidentally interpreted as flags for the `nix run` command itself.

use serde_json::Value;
use std::io::{self, BufRead};

fn main() {
    let stdin = io::stdin();
    for line in stdin.lock().lines() {
        let line = match line {
            Ok(l) => l,
            Err(_) => continue,
        };
        let trimmed = line.trim();
        if trimmed.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }

        match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(trimmed) {
            Ok(data) => {
                let output = format_entry(&data);
                println!("{}", output);
            }
            Err(_) => {
                let preview = if trimmed.len() > 50 { &trimmed[..50] } else { trimmed };
                eprintln!("Skipping invalid JSON line: {}...", preview);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn format_entry(data: &Value) -> String {
    let mut output = String::new();
    
    if let Some(obj) = data.as_object() {
        let id = obj.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("N/A");
        output.push_str(&format!("## Entry {}\n\n", id));
        
        output.push_str(&format_value(data, 0));
        
        output.push_str("---\n\n");
    }
    
    output
}

fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        Value::Array(arr) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            for (i, val) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
                output.push_str(&format!("[] {}\n\n", i));
                if val.is_object() || val.is_array() {
                    output.push_str(&format_value(val, indent + 1));
                } else {
                    output.push_str(&format_leaf(val));
                    output.push_str("\n\n");
                }
            }
            output
        }
        _ => format_leaf(value),
    }
}

fn format_leaf(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
        Value::Number(n) => n.to_string(),
        Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
        Value::Null => "null".to_string(),
        _ => serde_json::to_string(value).unwrap(),
    }
}

{
  description = "Bun Flake";

  inputs = {
    nixpkgs.url = "github:NixOS/nixpkgs/master";
  };

  outputs = { self, nixpkgs }:
    let
      supportedSystems = [
        "x86_64-linux"
        "aarch64-linux"
        "aarch64-darwin"
        "x86_64-darwin"
      ];
      forAllSystems = nixpkgs.lib.genAttrs supportedSystems;
    in {
      packages = forAllSystems (system: {
        default = nixpkgs.legacyPackages.${system}.bun;
      });
    };
}

#5 preamble

You are an expert coding assistant operating inside pi, a coding agent harness. You help users by reading files, executing commands, editing code, and writing new files.

#5 rules

<rules>
- Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find
- Use read to examine files instead of cat or sed.
- You can inspect PI_* environment variables for current model and session details.
- Use edit for precise changes (edits[].oldText must match exactly)
- When changing multiple separate locations in one file, use one edit call with multiple entries in edits[] instead of multiple edit calls
- Each edits[].oldText is matched against the original file, not after earlier edits are applied. Do not emit overlapping or nested edits. Merge nearby changes into one edit.
- Keep edits[].oldText as small as possible while still being unique in the file. Do not pad with large unchanged regions.
- Use write only for new files or complete rewrites.
- Be concise in your responses
- Show file paths clearly when working with files
</rules>

#5 tools

<tools>
- read: Read file contents
- bash: Execute bash commands (ls, grep, find, etc.)
- edit: Make precise file edits with exact text replacement, including multiple disjoint edits in one call
- write: Create or overwrite files

In addition to the tools above, you may have access to other custom tools depending on the project.
</tools>

#6 thinking

The user wants to know how to run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`.

The `nix run ./bun` command was executed, and it showed the help message for `bun`.
The `bun/flake.nix` file defines a package that provides the `bun` binary.

When using `nix run`, you can pass arguments to the binary using `--` to separate `nix run` arguments from the binary arguments.

So, to run `bun install`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- install`.
To run `bun run --version`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- run --version`.

Wait, I should verify this behavior with a small test or just explain it.
Actually, I can just tell the user.

Let's double check.
If I run `nix run ./bun -- info`, it should execute `bun info`.

I'll provide examples.

#6 thinkingSignature

reasoning_content

#6 type

thinking

[] 1

#6 text

To run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`, you should use the `--` separator. This tells Nix to stop parsing arguments for `nix run` and pass everything following the separator directly to the `bun` binary.

Here are some examples:

**1. Running a subcommand (e.g., `bun install`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- install
```

**2. Running a script with arguments (e.g., `bun run -- some-script.ts`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- run -- some-script.ts
```

**3. Checking the version:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- --version
```

**4. Adding a dependency:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- add <package-name>
```

**Why use `--`?**
While `nix run` often passes trailing arguments to the binary automatically, using `--` is the standard and safest way to ensure that flags intended for `bun` (like `-v` or `--help`) are not accidentally interpreted as flags for the `nix run` command itself.

{
  description = "Bun Flake";

  inputs = {
    nixpkgs.url = "github:NixOS/nixpkgs/master";
  };

  outputs = { self, nixpkgs }:
    let
      supportedSystems = [
        "x86_64-linux"
        "aarch64-linux"
        "aarch64-darwin"
        "x86_64-darwin"
      ];
      forAllSystems = nixpkgs.lib.genAttrs supportedSystems;
    in {
      packages = forAllSystems (system: {
        default = nixpkgs.legacyPackages.${system}.bun;
      });
    };
}

</docs>

#5 preamble

You are an expert coding assistant operating inside pi, a coding agent harness. You help users by reading files, executing commands, editing code, and writing new files.

#5 rules

<rules>
- Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find
- Use read to examine files instead of cat or sed.
- You can inspect PI_* environment variables for current model and session details.
- Use edit for precise changes (edits[].oldText must match exactly)
- When changing multiple separate locations in one file, use one edit call with multiple entries in edits[] instead of multiple edit calls
- Each edits[].oldText is matched against the original file, not after earlier edits are applied. Do not emit overlapping or nested edits. Merge nearby changes into one edit.
- Keep edits[].oldText as small as possible while still being unique in the file. Do not pad with large unchanged regions.
- Use write only for new files or complete rewrites.
- Be concise in your responses
- Show file paths clearly when working with files
</rules>

#5 tools

<tools>
- read: Read file contents
- bash: Execute bash commands (ls, grep, find, etc.)
- edit: Make precise file edits with exact text replacement, including multiple disjoint edits in one call
- write: Create or overwrite files

In addition to the tools above, you may have access to other custom tools depending on the project.
</tools>

#6 thinking

The user wants to know how to run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`.

The `nix run ./bun` command was executed, and it showed the help message for `bun`.
The `bun/flake.nix` file defines a package that provides the `bun` binary.

When using `nix run`, you can pass arguments to the binary using `--` to separate `nix run` arguments from the binary arguments.

So, to run `bun install`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- install`.
To run `bun run --version`, you would use `nix run ./bun -- run --version`.

Wait, I should verify this behavior with a small test or just explain it.
Actually, I can just tell the user.

Let's double check.
If I run `nix run ./bun -- info`, it should execute `bun info`.

I'll provide examples.

#6 thinkingSignature

reasoning_content

#6 type

thinking

[] 1

#6 text

To run the `bun` command with subcommands and options using `nix run ./bun`, you should use the `--` separator. This tells Nix to stop parsing arguments for `nix run` and pass everything following the separator directly to the `bun` binary.

Here are some examples:

**1. Running a subcommand (e.g., `bun install`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- install
```

**2. Running a script with arguments (e.g., `bun run -- some-script.ts`):**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- run -- some-script.ts
```

**3. Checking the version:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- --version
```

**4. Adding a dependency:**
```bash
nix run ./bun -- add <package-name>
```

**Why use `--`?**
While `nix run` often passes trailing arguments to the binary automatically, using `--` is the standard and safest way to ensure that flags intended for `bun` (like `-v` or `--help`) are not accidentally interpreted as flags for the `nix run` command itself.

The output of `cat 2026-10-08T12-27-34-074Z_01a11b7b-b339-7234-a455-b48c201c54b6.md` shows:

```markdown

01a11b7b-b339-7234-a455-b48c201c54b6

#3 timestamp

session

#3 version

3

---
```

The keys are rendered as `#3 cwd`, `#3 id`, etc.
Wait, the user wanted "the key itself on its own line".
My current implementation prints:
`#3 cwd`

`#3 id`

The header level is 3.
The key is "cwd".
So it's `### cwd`.

Wait, in the output, it's `#3 cwd`.
The `#3` part comes from `header_level`.
Wait, `3 + 0 = 3`.
So `header_level` is 3.
`#3` is the Markdown header of level 3.

Wait, why did it print `#3 cwd`?
The code is:
`output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));`
So if `header_level` is 3 and `key` is "cwd", it should be `### cwd`.

Wait, I see `#3 cwd` in the output.
That means `header_level` is being interpreted as `3`.
Wait, `#3` in Markdown is a header of level 3.
But it looks like it's printing the digit `3` as part of the header.

Ah, I see what's wrong.
The `header_level` is 3.
`#3` is a header level 3.
But `###` is the correct way to write a header level 3 in Markdown.

So `header_level` should be used to generate the correct number of `#` symbols.

I need to change `format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key)` to something that prints the correct number of `#`.

Wait, `header_level` is an integer.
If `header_level` is 3, I want `###`.

So I should use `"#".repeat(header_level)`.

Let's check the current `format_value` code again.
```rust
fn format_value(value: &Value, indent: usize) -> String {
    let header_level = 3 + indent;
    match value {
        Value::Object(map) => {
            let mut output = String::new();
            let mut keys: Vec<_> = map.keys().collect();
            keys.sort();
            for key in keys {
                let val = &map[key];
                output.push_str(&format!("#{} {}\n\n", header_level, key));
                // ...
```

Yes, `format!("#{} {}", 3, "cwd")` produces `### cwd`.
Wait, why did it produce `#3 cwd`?
Ah, maybe `header_level` is being treated as a variable name? No, it's an integer.

Wait, if `header_level` is 3, then `format!("#{} {}", 3, "cwd")` produces `### cwd`.
If it produced `#3 cwd`, then `header_level` must have been `3` and the first `#` was literal.

Wait, `#3 cwd` means a header of level 1 followed by the text `3 cwd`.
Because `#` followed by a digit is a header level.
Wait, no.
`#` is level 1.
`##` is level 2.
`###` is level 3.

So `### cwd` is a level 3 header.
If the output is `#3 cwd`, then it's a level 1 header with the text `3 cwd`.

Wait, why would it be `3 cwd`?
If `header_level` is 3.
Then `format!("#{} {}", 3, "cwd")` should produce `### cwd`.

Wait, I think I know what happened.
Maybe `header_level` was not 3?

Actually, I'll just fix it to use `.repeat()`.
